Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um... I hope I'm not wasting your time, it's probably not a huge deal. But, well, honestly... what made me come in is that I've been having a little bit of trouble at work. 

I'm an advertising sales agent, and we have these really long strategy meetings. And I just... I keep getting up during them. I know I'm supposed to just stay seated like everyone else, but I always end up popping up out of my chair. I try to make it look completely normal! Like, I'll just go refill my water, or I'll kind of pace over by the printer for a minute just to move. 

It's nothing disruptive, I promise. But I know it probably doesn't look incredibly professional to my manager when I can't just sit through the meeting. Does that make sense? I just thought I should maybe ask someone about it, if that's okay.

Um, no, I don't really think so. At least, I haven't noticed it if I do. When I'm at home, I'm usually pretty... pretty normal, I think.

Um, well... I guess I did have a little trouble with that when I was younger, yeah. I mean, it wasn't anything terrible! I was always a good kid, I promise. But... I do remember this happening way back, like, way before high school even. 

My elementary school teachers would always write on my report cards that I was really bright, which was nice! But they'd also say I... I rushed through my work a bit, or that I talked out of turn sometimes. And I guess I was a little forgetful with my homework, and my desk was always kind of a mess, just stuffed full of half-finished papers and things.

Um, well... I try really hard to keep things together at home, I really do! I'm definitely not a total mess or anything, I promise. But I guess... well, my organization can get just a little bit... creative sometimes? 

Like, the lines blur a bit between everything I'm juggling, and my system gets a little scattered. For example, my campaign notes for my accounts will somehow end up split between my emails, a bunch of sticky notes all over my desk, my physical planner, and just... so many random tabs open on my laptop at home. 

And then I sit there and... well, I guess I do waste a lot of time just trying to figure out what to do first instead of just starting the task. 

I always get everything done eventually, though! I just get a little overwhelmed trying to prioritize it all when it's spread out like that. I'm sure a lot of people have too many tabs open and use too many sticky notes these days, right? Everyone is just so busy. I hope that doesn't make my life sound too chaotic... is that the sort of thing you meant?

Um... impulsivity? Gosh, that sounds a little intense. I definitely don't do anything reckless or dangerous, I promise! I'm actually a very careful person. 

But, well... I guess if you mean just little things at work? Sometimes I can be a little bit... overly enthusiastic, maybe? Like, if one of my coworkers is working through a pitch deck, and I suddenly think of an idea for it... I have this habit where I'll just sort of pop over to their desk right then. Or, um, if they're in the middle of a conversation with someone else, I might accidentally interrupt them just to add my thought. 

I always mean well! I just get so excited to help, and I want to share the idea before it slips my mind. But... I guess, if I'm being honest, it probably could have waited. I always feel a little bad afterward, and I make sure to apologize! I try really hard to be polite and respectful of everyone's time, I really do. 

Does... does that sound like what you're asking about? I really hope my team doesn't think I'm rude... I just want to be a good collaborator.

through_Difficulty\n  description: Often does not follow through on instructions and fails to finish schoolwork, chores, or duties in the workplace.\n  manifestation: I’ll get most of a proposal or description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: My campaign notes end up split between emails, sticky notes, my planner, and random tabs, and I waste a lot of time figuring out what to do first instead of just starting.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for things I need for work, like my headset, client one-pagers, keys, or the notebook I was using ten minutes earlier.\n- key: S011\n  name: Leaving_Seat\n  description: Often leaves seat in before a question has been completed.\n  manifestation: I often jump in with an answer before a client or coworker has finished asking the question, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing or cut them off.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting my turn on client calls or in office discussions makes me restless, so I keep checking the clock or rehearsing what I want to say until I blurt it out too early.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: If I think of an idea while someone else is working through a deck, I’ll pop over or interrupt their advertising_sales_agent / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S011\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Did you experience similar difficulties with restlessness and paying attention during your teenage years, after elementary school?\n\nPatient: Um, well... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I definitely don't think I'm running around the room or acting like a little kid anymore, I promise! I've definitely grown up a lot since elementary school. 

But I guess... well, some of the things we've been talking about, like the scattered notes and getting a little too eager to share ideas... I suppose those might be kind of similar? Just in a more grown-up way? 

If I really think about it, I guess I've been noticing these things a bit more recently. Like, it hasn't always been this much of a struggle. I'd say it's really been noticeably affecting me since I changed into advertising sales strategy... which was about two years and two months ago, I think? 

It's just a lot more independent work and juggling different accounts, so I think maybe it just requires a different kind of focus than my old role. I'm really trying my best to adapt, though! I want to do a good job. Do you think it's normal for a big career change to bring out some of these little quirks again? I just hope I don't sound like I'm failing at my job, because I'm really not.

I suppose those might be kind of similar? Just in a more grown-up way? \n\nIf I really think about it, I guess I've been noticing these things a bit more recently. Like, it hasn't always been this much of a struggle. I'd say it's really been noticeably affecting me since I changed into advertising sales strategy... which was about two years and two months ago, I think? \n\nIt's just a lot more independent work and juggling different accounts, so I think maybe it just requires a different kind of focus than my old role. I'm really trying my best to adapt, though! I want to do a good job. Do you think it's normal for a big career change to bring out some of these little quirks again? I just hope I don't sound like I'm failing at my job, because I'm really not.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any of these difficulties with restlessness, paying attention, or impulsivity consistently over the past few years, or have they varied over time?\n\nPatient: Um... consistently? Gosh, I don't know if I'd say it's been a constant, everyday kind of thing. I really hope it doesn't sound like I've just been struggling non-stop this whole time! \n\nIt definitely varies. Like, some weeks are completely fine, and I feel totally on top of everything at work and at home. But then... well, maybe if things get really busy, or if I have a lot of different accounts pulling me in different directions, that's when I might get a little more scattered or... or jumpy, I guess? \n\nLike I mentioned, before I took this specific role a couple of years ago, I don't think I really noticed it much at all. So I suppose it just fluctuates depending on how much I'm juggling at any given moment? \n\nDoes that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer... I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I've definitely had plenty of really good, productive times too, I promise!\n\n[Last Question]\nHave these difficulties with restlessness, paying attention, or impulsivity significantly impacted your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 30 / advertising_sales_agent / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"At work, it has started showing up in performance reviews and client trust; one client complaint especially made me worry that I could damage accounts even though I care a lot about doing good work.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... significantly impacted? That feels like a really strong word. I definitely don't want you to think my life is falling apart or anything! My relationships are great, my friends are wonderful, and I really do love my job. 

But... I guess, if I'm being totally transparent... there have been a couple of tiny hiccups at work. Nothing major, I promise! Just... well, my manager gently brought up a few things in my last performance review. Just about, you know, staying organized and making sure I'm fully present in meetings. 

And, um... there was this one little incident with a client. I guess they felt like I wasn't listening closely enough to their needs, and they actually complained. That honestly broke my heart a little bit, because I care so much about doing a good job for them. I was just so worried that I might accidentally damage an account, even though I'm trying my absolute hardest. 

I've been working really hard to fix it, though! I'm trying all sorts of new planners and reminders. I just... I really want to be seen as a reliable employee. Does that sound like the kind of thing you're asking about? I hope it doesn't make me sound like a terrible worker, because I really do put my all into it!

That feels like a really strong word. I definitely don't want you to think my life is falling apart or anything! My relationships are great, my friends are wonderful, and I really do love my job. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being totally transparent... there have been a couple of tiny hiccups at work. Nothing major, I promise! Just... well, my manager gently brought up a few things in my last performance review. Just about, you know, staying organized and making sure I'm fully present in meetings. \n\nAnd, um... there was this one little incident with a client. I guess they felt like I wasn't listening closely enough to their needs, and they actually complained. I was just so worried that I might accidentally damage an account, even though I'm trying my absolute hardest. \n\nI've been working really hard to fix it, though! I'm trying all sorts of new planners and reminders. I just... I really want to be seen as a reliable employee. Does that sound like the kind of thing you're asking about? I hope it doesn't make me sound like a terrible worker, because I really do put my all into it!\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any symptoms of restlessness, difficulty paying attention, or impulsivity since you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Um, well... since I was a teenager? Gosh, I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... it actually goes back even further than that, I think? \n\nI know we touched on this a little bit earlier, but... yeah, my elementary school teachers used to leave these little notes on my report cards. They were always really nice about it! They'd say I was bright and enthusiastic, which was sweet of them. But they also mentioned that I tended to rush through things, or... or maybe talk out of turn sometimes when I got too excited. \n\nAnd, um, I guess I had a bit of a habit of forgetting my homework back then, too. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, I don't think so! I mean, I really value my friendships, and I've always tried my best to be a good friend. I hope I am, anyway! 

I guess... well, maybe sometimes I might accidentally interrupt someone if I get really excited about a story they're telling, or... or I might be a tiny bit late to lunch because I lost track of time. But my friends are so incredibly understanding, and they know I never mean anything by it. I always apologize right away, and I make sure to let them know how much I appreciate them. 

So, no, I haven't really had trouble making or keeping friends. I'm actually really lucky to have a great group of people in my life! I hope that's a good thing to hear... I just really want to be a supportive person to the people I care about, you know?

Like, I'll just go refill my water, or I'll kind of pace over by the printer for a minute just to move. \n\nIt's nothing disruptive, I promise. But I know it probably doesn't look incredibly professional to my manager when I can't just sit through the meeting. Does that make sense? I just thought I should maybe ask someone about it, if that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not in meetings, like when you're at home or doing other activities?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really think so. At least, I haven't noticed it if I do. When I'm at home, I'm usually pretty... pretty normal, I think. \n\nI mean, I might tidy up or keep my hands busy while I watch TV or something, but everyone kind of does that, right? I don't think I'm pacing around my living room or doing anything weird. I really try to just relax when I'm off the clock, so I can be at my best for work the next day. \n\nI hope that's helpful? I just... I don't want to make it sound like I'm bouncing off the walls all the time, because I'm really not. It's mostly just those long work meetings where I feel like I'm doing something wrong. Is it okay that it's just happening at work?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention during those long work meetings?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try my best to stay engaged, I promise. But... yeah, in those longer strategy meetings, or even if I'm just reading a long client brief, I'll start out completely focused. I really do. \n\nBut then, after a few minutes, I'll sort of realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide, or the same paragraph, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. \n\nI always catch myself and try to get right back on track, though! I really don't want you to think I'm ignoring my coworkers or doing a bad job, because I care a lot about my work. It's just a little blip where my mind drifts. Does that sound like something a lot of people struggle with? I'm hoping it's pretty normal to zone out a little when things get long... right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you're in those long work meetings?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no. No, I don't think so. I actually really like my team, and the meetings themselves aren't really high-pressure or anything. I mean, my manager is great, so I definitely don't feel intimidated or... or anxious about being there. \n\nIt's really just... I just get that little urge to move around, or my mind just kind of drifts, like I mentioned. But I'm not sitting there worrying or feeling stressed out at all. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. Is it... is it okay that it happens even when I'm completely relaxed?\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or paying attention when you were a kid in school?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I did have a little trouble with that when I was younger, yeah. I mean, it wasn't anything terrible! I was always a good kid, I promise. But... I do remember this happening way back, like, way before high school even. \n\nMy elementary school teachers would always write on my report cards that I was really bright, which was nice! But they'd also say I... I rushed through my work a bit, or that I talked out of turn sometimes. And I guess I was a little forgetful with my homework, and my desk was always kind of a mess, just stuffed full of half-finished papers and things. \n\nI always meant to be organized and quiet, I really did. I just... I got a little too enthusiastic sometimes, I think? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad student. A lot of kids are a little disorganized and talkative at that age, right? Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble keeping a job or getting along with friends because of your restlessness or difficulty paying attention?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I wouldn't say I've ever had trouble getting along with friends! My friends are wonderful, and we get along really well. I try really hard to be a good friend, I promise. \n\nAs for work... um, I definitely haven't ever lost a job or anything like that! I've always been able to keep my jobs. I do really care about doing good work for my clients. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe recently... it has been mentioned just a tiny bit in my performance reviews? Nothing awful! Just... you know, some little notes about maybe missing small details. And... well, there was one client who... they were just a little frustrated because I missed something in an email thread, and they complained. \n\nIt really wasn't a huge deal, but it did make me worry a little bit. I would hate to damage any accounts, because I really do try so hard to keep everyone happy and do my best. I just... I get a little scattered sometimes, I guess? I hope you don't think I'm a bad employee. It's just a little hiccup, right? A lot of people probably get minor complaints sometimes...?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience similar difficulties with restlessness and paying attention during your teenage years, after elementary school?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I didn't really notice it as much in middle school or high school. I mean, I don't think I did? I was really involved in a lot of clubs and things, so I was always pretty busy. \n\nI don't remember getting into any trouble for talking or moving around too much back then. I just don't want to make it sound like I was totally out of control or anything, because I really wasn't.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find yourself rushing through tasks or having trouble with organization in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... Like, if one of my coworkers is working through a pitch deck, and I suddenly think of an idea for it... I have this habit where I'll just sort of pop over to their desk right then. Or, um, if they're in the middle of a conversation with someone else, I might accidentally interrupt them just to add my thought. \n\nI always mean well! I just get so excited to help, and I want to share the idea before it slips my mind. But... I guess, if I'm being honest, it probably could have waited. I always feel a little bad afterward, and I make sure to apologize! I try really hard to be polite and respectful of everyone's time, I really do. \n\nDoes... does that sound like what you're asking about? I really hope my team doesn't think I'm rude... I just want to be a good collaborator.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've grown out of the behaviors your elementary school teachers noted, or do you still find yourself exhibiting similar traits as an adult?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely don't think I'm running around the room or acting like a little kid anymore, I promise! I've definitely grown up a lot since elementary school. \n\nBut I guess... well, some of the things we've been talking about, like the scattered notes and getting a little too eager to share ideas... I suppose those might be kind of similar? Just in a more grown-up way? \n\nIf I really think about it, I guess I've been noticing these things a bit more recently. Like, it hasn't always been this much of a struggle. I'd say it's really been noticeably affecting me since I changed into advertising sales strategy... which was about two years and two months ago, I think? \n\nIt's just a lot more independent work and juggling different accounts, so I think maybe it just requires a different kind of focus than my old role. I'm really trying my best to adapt, though! I want to do a good job. Do you think it's normal for a big career change to bring out some of these little quirks again? I just hope I don't sound like I'm failing at my job, because I'm really not.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any of these difficulties with restlessness, paying attention, or impulsivity consistently over the past few years, or have they varied over time?\n\nPatient: Um... consistently? Gosh, I don't know if I'd say it's been a constant, everyday kind of thing. I really hope it doesn't sound like I've just been struggling non-stop this whole time! \n\nIt definitely varies. Like, some weeks are completely fine, and I feel totally on top of everything at work and at home. But then... well, maybe if things get really busy, or if I have a lot of different accounts pulling me in different directions, that's when I might get a little more scattered or... or jumpy, I guess? \n\nLike I mentioned, before I took this specific role a couple of years ago, I don't think I really noticed it much at all. Um, well... I know I just talked about this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much! You're so patient to listen to me ramble. But yes, I guess if I look back, it really has been there since way before high school.

Is it... is it okay that it happens even when I'm completely relaxed?\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or paying attention when you were a kid in school?\n\nPatient: Um, well... But they'd also say I... I rushed through my work a bit, or that I talked out of turn sometimes. And I guess I was a little forgetful with my homework, and my desk was always kind of a mess, just stuffed full of half-finished papers and things. \n\nI always meant to be organized and quiet, I really did. I just... I got a little too enthusiastic sometimes, I think? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad student. A lot of kids are a little disorganized and talkative at that age, right? Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble keeping a job or getting along with friends because of your restlessness or difficulty paying attention?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I wouldn't say I've ever had trouble getting along with friends! My friends are wonderful, and we get along really well. I try really hard to be a good friend, I promise. \n\nAs for work... um, I definitely haven't ever lost a job or anything like that! I've always been able to keep my jobs. I do really care about doing good work for my clients. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe recently... it has been mentioned just a tiny bit in my performance reviews? Nothing awful! Just... you know, some little notes about maybe missing small details. And... well, there was one client who... they were just a little frustrated because I missed something in an email thread, and they complained. \n\nIt really wasn't a huge deal, but it did make me worry a little bit. I would hate to damage any accounts, because I really do try so hard to keep everyone happy and do my best. I just... I get a little scattered sometimes, I guess? I hope you don't think I'm a bad employee. It's just a little hiccup, right? A lot of people probably get minor complaints sometimes...?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience similar difficulties with restlessness and paying attention during your teenage years, after elementary school?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I didn't really notice it as much in middle school or high school. I mean, I don't think I did? I was really involved in a lot of clubs and things, so I was always pretty busy. \n\nI don't remember getting into any trouble for talking or moving around too much back then. I think I kind of figured out how to sit quietly by that point, you know? And I got decent grades! I really tried my best to stay on top of things. \n\nIf I'm being completely honest, maybe I did still struggle a little bit with... just keeping track of all my assignments? But it wasn't anything major, I promise. I think a lot of teenagers probably have a hard time juggling all their classes, right? Did I answer that okay? I just don't want to make it sound like I was totally out of control or anything, because I really wasn't.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find yourself rushing through tasks or having trouble with organization in your daily life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I try really hard to keep things together at home, I really do! I'm definitely not a total mess or anything, I promise. But I guess... well, my organization can get just a little bit... creative sometimes? \n\nLike, the lines blur a bit between everything I'm juggling, and my system gets a little scattered. Gosh, I don't know if I'd say it's been a constant, everyday kind of thing. I really hope it doesn't sound like I've just been struggling non-stop this whole time! \n\nIt definitely varies. Like, some weeks are completely fine, and I feel totally on top of everything at work and at home. I guess if you mean just little things at work? Sometimes I can be a little bit... overly enthusiastic, maybe? Like, if one of my coworkers is working through a pitch deck, and I suddenly think of an idea for it... I have this habit where I'll just sort of pop over to their desk right then. Or, um, if they're in the middle of a conversation with someone else, I might accidentally interrupt them just to add my thought. \n\nI always mean well! I just get so excited to help, and I want to share the idea before it slips my mind. But... I guess, if I'm being honest, it probably could have waited. I always feel a little bad afterward, and I make sure to apologize! I try really hard to be polite and respectful of everyone's time, I really do. \n\nDoes... does that sound like what you're asking about? I really hope my team doesn't think I'm rude... I just want to be a good collaborator.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've grown out of the behaviors your elementary school teachers noted, or do you still find yourself exhibiting similar traits as an adult?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely don't think I'm running around the room or acting like a little kid anymore, I promise! I've definitely grown up a lot since elementary school. \n\nBut I guess... well, some of the things we've been talking about, like the scattered notes and getting a little too eager to share ideas... I suppose those might be kind of similar? Just in a more grown-up way? \n\nIf I really think about it, I guess I've been noticing these things a bit more recently. I'm really trying my best to adapt, though! I want to do a good job. Do you think it's normal for a big career change to bring out some of these little quirks again? I just hope I don't sound like I'm failing at my job, because I'm really not.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any of these difficulties with restlessness, paying attention, or impulsivity consistently over the past few years, or have they varied over time?\n\nPatient: Um... consistently? Gosh, I don't know if I'd say it's been a constant, everyday kind of thing. I really hope it doesn't sound like I've just been struggling non-stop this whole time! \n\nIt definitely varies. Like, some weeks are completely fine, and I feel totally on top of everything at work and at home. Like, some weeks are completely fine, and I feel totally on top of everything at work and at home.
